fWo — Card

Best Of 2000 Show, 24 December 2000

24 December 2000
Content noticeA period document, served unedited. It carries the humor, language, and content typical of its era and community — some of it crude, dated, or offensive, including material that wouldn't be written today. See Curation.
Chapter One: January-April The scene opened on fWo play-by-play announcer Jeremiah Frankton; who sat in what appeared to be a editing studio. With a bank of television monitors behind him, Frankton welcomed everyone to the fWo "Best Of 2000" show. "Hello, and welcome to a very special edition of Sunday Night Slaughter - the final Slaughter of 2000; as a matter of fact. In the next six hours, we will take a very special look back at the year that was 2000, and bring you only the best moments. We will be showing fifteen matches, with most of them in their entirety. Given that we're just hours away from celebrating Christmas tomorrow, we'd like to show our appreciation to our fans by giving them matches they may or may not have seen over the last year... right here, free, tonight. One match from our most recent pay-per-view - Twilight - will also be shown here tonight. We've got a lot to cover, so let's get right into it. Sit back and spend your Christmas Eve with the fans Wrestling organization." Frankton gave a beaming smile, before a graphic splashed on the screen. The graphic read "CHAPTER ONE", with the following text below it. CHAPTER ONE: January-April We'll make it up to you In the year 2000 Build it up for you In the year 2000 Make it hard for you In the year 2000. -"Anthem For The Year 2000", Silverchair The graphic disappeared from the screen to reveal Frankton standing now in front of a single large television screen. "Fans, if I could use one word to sum up this past year, it'd be shocking. And really, this year began in a truly shocking way on the very first show of 2000. Just take a look at this." Frankton moved out of the way of the screen, and the following videotape began to roll: Wednesday Warfare - January 5, 2000 "Beautiful People" by Marilyn Manson began to play throughout the arena. The casket opened up, and a figure sat up inside. He climbed out, but nobody knew who the person was as they wore a black trenchcoat, a black shirt, black pants and black boots. The person began to walk to the ring methodically. As Universal and Panther were about to go meet the person in the aisleway, Scott Slugger came into the ring behind them through the crowd. He whacked Universal and Panther each over the head with a steel chair, before he took out the Panther with a elevated reverse DDT. As the masked man reached ringside, Dawn moved towards him and he pushed her aside via a hand to the face. The masked man grabbed Lance Star and pushed the him into the guardrail while he was in his wheel chair. In the ring Heaven (Fyre), Living (The Destroyer) and Hell (Famine) pounded on Universal on the mat, while Slugger stomped Panther repeatedly. The dark figure stepped up onto the ring steps and outstretched his arms in a crucifixion pose, before raising his arms into the air. A strobe light began to flicker throughout the arena while his music continued to place. He entered the ring as Heaven/Living/Hell brought Universal to his feet. The crowd booed loudly as he did a throat slash gesture, before kicking Universal in the stomach, followed by a sitdown tombstone piledriver. Slugger and the masked man dragged Panther on top of Universal, before they woke up the referee and had him count the pinfall. Three seconds later, and the Panther remained the fWo World Champion. Hell went to the outside and retrieved the World Title, before he brought it into the ring. Slugger grabbed the referee and threw him over the top rope out onto the floor. The arena darkened as Hell went to his knees, kneeling in front of the masked man with the title in a grandoise salute to Him. He held up the title to him while a spotlight shined on the masked man. The masked man took off his mask... and moments later... the world saw who it was. Austin Davis had returned. The second coming had arrived. Davis snatched the fWo World Title from Hell, and he looked at it. He spit on the title, before holding it in one hand above the fallen bodies of Universal and Panther. Slugger held the other part of the title with a single hand as well, as their friends knelt before them. Davis and Slugger dropped the title onto the Panther, while "DEAD" and "PERFECT" burst onto the videoscreen. As Davis and Slugger stood in the ring over the two men, the two words transformed into three. "Fuck the fWo." "Back in July of 1999 at the first annual fWo Summer Slaughter pay-per-view", Frankton said; "Machina defeated Austin Davis in match that could be best described as "Control versus Career". At the time, Davis was embroiled in a feud with the Commissioner of the fWo, one Travis Beaven. Davis wanted control of the fWo... but Beaven wasn't ready to give it to a man who was evil & vile such as Davis. Davis offered to put up his career, if Beaven would put up his company in a match at Summer Slaughter. Beaven agreed, only he wouldn't wrestle in the actual match itself... he would have someone wrestle for him. Possible opponents for Davis ranged from Jim Dudley to Stinky to Briana to yes, the 1991 Denver Broncos. Everyone was truly fooled when it was Machina, a man who left the fWo in a very confusing way, and was not on the best of terms with Beaven either. Machina defeated Davis, and Davis kept his word and retired. Last year at the Countdown shows, the fWo became a company full of turmoil, as Beaven had clearly lost control of his own organization. Beaven left... and Davis saw it was his time to return with Beaven out of the picture. Immediately, Davis gained the trust of Chris Finn & James Armstrong, who were acting in the place of Beaven, and were running the company. Everyone knew you shouldn't trust Austin Davis... but Finn & Armstrong were convinced he had changed his ways - until the manner in which Davis returned, of which we just saw. Davis immediately targeted to control the company... and he did have a degree of power. As for Beaven, Beaven apparently passed away in January at some point... although some today still believe that he's actually alive on some mountain somewhere in hiding, living the good life." Frankton reached for a cup of ice tea, before he continued. "With the first Warfare completed, 2000 was truly off and running. Davis was shockingly helped in his quest to "kill" the fWo by Scott Slugger, who had changed his name to Perfect, while Davis was going by Dead. The two ran the fWo for several shows, until their power ended the day after Invasion! - as Dead was defeated in another control versus career match... only this time he didn't lose his career, as it was the career of The Boston Strangler that was on the line. During this time, The Black Panther was the fWo World Champion, and he was targeted by The Flying Frenchie. It was a rematch from their classic encounter in the Wrestler of the Year finals at Fallout 1999, which saw The Black Panther win. The difference in this match was the "home field" advantage the Frenchie held, as this match took place over in the country of France. Undoubtedly, having his hometown fans behind him helped him pick up the win - but let's show you how the Frenchie won his third - and final - fWo World Title. Let's take you to Chris Finn & Stevie Roberts for the call of the action." End Of the Invasion! fWo World Title Match - Panther vs. Frenchi Finn: This has been a long and grueling match, and that incredible move a moment ago took a hell of a lot out of both men. The referee beginning to count both men down here... Ref: 1... Ref: 2... Ref: 3... Ref: 4... Ref: 5! Ref: 6! Ref: 7! Finn: Wait...Panther is dragging himself up...and so is Frenchie! Finn: Both men face each other...and both men collapse once again! Finn: Unbelievable! Finn: The ref starts counting again, but now it looks like Panther and Frenchie are getting back up...right attempted by Panther, blocked by Frenchie who responds with a left, block by Panther, quick double-underhook and a suplex! Finn: Panther rolls onto Frenchie... Finn: 1! Finn: 2! Finn: Thre- no! Frenchie kicks out, and Panther dragging him up. Backing Frenchie up into the corner... Both men drenched with sweat, and now the sweat flying off Frenchie and into the crowd as Panther blasts his head repeatedly into the turnbuckles! Finn: Frenchie staggers out...throws a wild punch that misses by a mile...he's down on his feet, but he refuses to go down! Finn: Panther winds up now...measures...and absolutely BLASTS Frenchie with a huge right! Finn: Frenchie takes it right on the skull...stands...and crumples backwards down to the mat! Frenchie splayed out in the middle of the ring...and Panther falls onto him! Almost a form of a senton there...and that is a pinning position! Finn: 1! Finn: 2! Finn: No! Roberts: Incredible, baby...these two men are going through hell and back just for that title. And to that I say... Roberts: Ha! All it took me was Cactus Jon, a cage, and a kneelock, baby! Finn: Be that as it may, both men are still giving their all here...and Panther is getting back up! Now he pulls Frenchie up as well...lifts him up...vertical suplex! He holds on...and another! Finn: Panther seems to be getting a second wind here...or a third or fourth wind...and he holds on and brings Frenchie up one more time...fishermans suplex! Finn: 1! Finn: 2! Finn: Kickout! Finn: Incredible! Frenchie just not willing to stay down here...and Panther brings him back up again. Whips him into the ropes, powerslam! Panther quickly brings him back to his feet...short whip, samoan drop! Finn: Cover! Finn: 1! Finn: 2! Finn: No! Finn: Panther cannot believe the resiliency of Frenchie here...brings him up again...and puts his head between his legs. Panther lifts Frenchie up...powerbomb! Finn: He's not satisfied! Panther is heading up top now...he rarely uses the top, except to hit the Pounce...and he looks off and measures Frenchie...leaps! Finn: Hits! Finn: A splash from Panther! Good God! Cover and a leg hook...one... Finn: Two... Finn: ... Finn: Good God No! The Frenchie almost superhuman here tonight! Finn: Panther has no idea what to do here...he's tried everything. Power, high-flying, extreme style, techincal...and Frenchie has absorbed all of it! Finn: Panther lifts Frenchie up for a gorilla press...he's got him up...Frenchie slips loose! Finn: He lands behind Panther...kick to the back of the right leg! Waistlock! German suplex! Finn: 1! Finn: 2! Finn: Kickout! Both men up quickly now, running on pure adrenaline...Panther runs at Frenchie, tries a clothesline- Finn: Frenchie ducks! Frenchie charges at Panther... Finn: Panther ducks a spinning heel kick! Wait! Frenchie landed on his feet! Like a cat! Roberts: I think he's looking to kill a cat here tonight, Finny! Finn: Dropkick to the back of the right knee! Panther down to one knee...quickly gets back up, and Frenchie leaps onto his back! Can Panther hold the weight of Frenchie? Finn: He's trying to throw Frenchie off here... Finn: Frenchie flips over into a standing diamond dust! I don't believe it! Frenchie dripping with sweat runs a thumb across his throat...He's headed up top! Finn: He's there! Finn: Flashbulbs popping all over here! Finn: GUILLOTINE LEGDROP! Finn: COVER! Finn: ONE! Finn: TWO! Finn: THREE! Finn: Three! The Flying Frenchie has done it! Frenchie has become a three-time fWo World Heavyweight Champion and the crowd here in France is going mad! Panzer: The winner of the match, and NEEEEW fans Wrestling organization World Heavyweight Champion, Ze Flying Frenchie! Finn: Frenchie still down on the mat from the beating he's taken...and you have to give Panther credit too. He gave and took one HELL of a lot of punishment. "And that is how the Flying Frenchie won his final World Title here in the fWo at Invasion! In other action that same night, the war over the fWo Hardcore Title continued as VL Tha Murdera and Hellrazor met in an epic Scaffold Match. Their war more or less began at Twilight of 1999 in a brutal Hardcore Match, and it continued over to the month of January. Everyone thought it would end at Invasion!, but it continued two months later in March... which we will certainly show you later on. For now, take a look at this brutality." Invasion! Tupac was groovy, baby... I always wondered if he pronounced his name "whoparksyourcar". Ever think about that, Finn-baby? When we return from the commercial break, we'll move onto February. Please, stick around." Frankton took another sip of his tea, as the "Best Of 2000" show went to a commercial break. Feb-00 If it makes you happy, it can't be that bad. If it makes you happy, then why the hell are you so sad? -"If It Makes You Happy", Sheryl Crow "Welcome back. Well, February can best be remembered for a number of things. Perhaps the return of Lance Star, who came back from a career threatening injury much sooner than expected. Or, perhaps it could be remembered for the brutal wars that had started up between Ice, Scott Collins & Scott Slugger. Perhaps it could also be remembered for the debut of Ric Chronos, who debuted that same month. Ric Chronos is the current fWo World Champion as of last Sunday; and in just under ten months, he has both endured & accomplished quite a bit. Perhaps it could also be remembered for the ongoing war between Dead & Chris Universal, which re-heated at the end of January, and raged on all throughout February. 'Mr. Main Event' Mitch Wilson also returned from his four month hiatus from the sport. Or... perhaps it could be remembered for some of the most hilarious stuff this sport has ever seen, courtesy of Machina & The Mad Trucker. Take a look." The following videotape would tell the story. Cola Clash 2000 - February 16, 2000 After a barrage of Coca Cola ads which included a nearly naked pre-teen girl sniffing the bubbles off a can of Coke, the lights in the arena went blue and the trademarked L in a circle wrote itself on the WarfareScreen. Machina appeared at the top of the entrance ramp, a bag filled with bottles of Coke in each hand and a Coca Cola hat on his masked head. He walked to the ring, opening the bottles up and spraying them into the crowd much to the dismay of owners of expensive electronic equipment. Once there he swung himself inside, saving one of the bottles of Coke for himself. He pulled a microphone from his tights, and smiling he looked around at the crowd. "Hello Baton Rouge!" The crowd responded with a mix of boos and cheers, and Machina sniffed the microphone suspiciously. Shrugging he continued. "You know, many of you many know me as a world class athlete and always tough competitor in and out of the ring. Sure, wrestling is my first love, but I kick some serious ass at Sorry." At the mention of the board game the crowd began to chant "ECW! ECW! ECW!" and Machina waited for them to settle themselves. When they did, he continued. "But what you might not know is that I couldn't have done any of it without the glorious taste of Coca Cola. It's always the right one, inside and outside of the ring. Available at the concession stand, it's always just the right thing to quench your thirst and make friends. Because Coca Cola isn't just a drink, it's a state of mind. Come on everyone... sing with me!" Machina pointed up to the Warfare Screen, where the spinning Coke logo had been replaced with song lyrics. With roughly half of the crowd following along, Machina sang, "I'd like to buy the world a Coke..." But before he could get any farther the Warfare Screen started to bend forward... and then it shattered outward. Cloth tore and metal crunched as an 18-wheeler emerged at the top of the entrance ramp. There was a moment of silence, and then... "TOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOT!" The door opened, and to the roar of the crowd the Mad Trucker exited the vehicle and pumped a fist into the air. With a large hose in one hand he let out another loud toot, and then pointed to Machina. "Listen here and shut your yapper! You keep talking about Coke... well I've got just one thing for you! Taste the Joy of Cola!" The Mad Trucker pointed the hose at the ring and Machina and turned a switch. Gallons of Pepsi suddenly surged into the ring, knocking Machina off his feet and coating the ring with the dark cola liquid. Machina tried to get to his feet and the Pepsi was aimed at him again, coating the Coca Cola spokesman with the contents of the truck... gallons and gallons of Pepsi. The Trucker finally dropped the hose and climbed atop his truck, once more pumping his fists into the air for the crowd. The crowd began to chant "Trucker! Trucker! Trucker!" And to the delight of the crowd another "TOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOT!" was ripped forth as Warfare went to commercial. February 20, 2000 The crowd shifted nervously, anxious for the next match to begin. Suddenly, the lights dimmed. Blue light began to flash, and smoke came from the top of the entrance ramp. "Come With Me" began to play, and the "L" of the Lone Gunmen blazed onto the SlaughterScreen. Machina stood at the top of the ramp, dressed in a Coke t-shirt, arms outstretched, and a bottle of Coke in each hand. As he walked to the ring fWo attendants suspended a can of Coke and a can of Pepsi on opposite corners of the ring, getting things ready for the upcoming match. Machina had made it to the ring, and was just starting to swing into the ring when the sound came. Ripping from the arena speakers like a message from God, fans rose to their feet and began to cheer. "TOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOOT!" The Mad Trucker came out onto the entrance ramp, and he wasn't alone. By his side was the adorable young girl who was known to Pepsi fans everywhere. With pigtails on each side of her head swaying, she walked cheerfully down to the ring with the Trucker. Machina watched them coming, his glare never leaving the face of the Trucker. On the Trucker's part he too was all business... eyes locked on Machina's and his usual jovial expression one of intensity and anger. For on the Trucker's part this was about Cola, and not a matter to be taken lightly. The Trucker neared the ring, and suddenly Machina's stoic form was broken as he ran across the ring and slid to the outside, catching the Trucker in the face with both boots. Machina shot an angry glare at the young girl at ringside who was looking at him stubbornly and then returned to the Trucker with a hard boot to the head. Signs of ring rust were obvious, as the Trucker was working to get back on his feet and get back into the smash mouth style of wrestling in the fWo. Machina looked up at the Pepsi can dangling high above the ring and considered going after it. He kicked at the Trucker once more and began to climb, just inches away from the prize, honor, and the match won. But before he could reach the can the Trucker was on his feet, on the apron, and had grabbed his leg, gripping it. Machina fought with him for a moment, both men high above the floor. Then Machina lost his grip, and the Trucker tossed Machina backward from the post and into the announcing table. The announcing table shattered, and Machina lay broken on the floor. Medical officials ran to ringside and worked to quickly get Machina's mask off to see if the blood coming from his mouth was the sign of anything more serious. The Mad Trucker looked on with some concern, for the moment forgetting the match at hand. They loaded Machina onto a gurney and began to take him away, the crowd cheering what they had just seen. The Trucker looked around the arena to see what he should do next, when the crowd began to make noise. Machina had gotten to his feet, snot coming out of his nose, and was limping his way back to the ring. The Trucker hopped off the apron and meet him at ringside with a big boot to the head that sent Machina spiraling backward. The two continued to brawl on the outside, the little girl cheering on the Trucker as they did. Machina, barely able to stand, held onto the metal apron for support as the Trucker climbed onto the ring apron. The Mad Trucker bellowed, and pitched himself from the apron toward Machina. But at that moment Machina noticed that his shoe had become untied during the crash through the table and he bent down to correct the problem. The Trucker hurtled over him, colliding with the metal barrier and falling to the ground in a heap. Machina looked over at him and smiled. He looked up at the can of Pepsi. All was clear for him, and he moved toward his goal. Suddenly he felt a small hand grabbing his leg, and he turned around. The little girl stood there, bottom lip stuck out stubbornly. Machina tried to shake her free, but she had a strong grip. "C'mon you little brat... get off'a me!" The little girl glared at Machina and her words came out in a deep throated growl. "Eat shit you bastard." Machina's eyes grew wide as the little girl grabbed him with her other arm as well, and then suplexed him off the ring apron and down to the arena floor. The little girl moved over to the steel steps and picked them up effortlessly, raising them above her head and moving over to Machina. Machina yelped, "Holy crap! Quick... bring out the bears! The bears now!" From the backstage area a growling was heard, and the two large Coke polar bears appeared at the top of the entrance ramp. Machina pointed to the young girl with the steel steps, and the polar bears loped toward her with jaws snarling. They fell upon her, ripping and tearing flesh from bone and sending a spray of blood out to the crowd. Machina got to his feet. "Ha ha! Take that you little bitch!" The bears heard him, and turned their attention to the strange masked man who was making strange loud noises. The bears growled and leapt toward him, and Machina quickly ran back up the entrance ramp for the backstage area, the two polar bears closely behind. By this time the Mad Trucker had regained his bearings, and got to his feet. Shaking off the cobwebs he looked down at the carcass of the young girl and shrugged. Rolling into the ring he climbed slowly up the turnbuckle post, and grabbed the Coke can, securing the victory. The crowd rose to their feet with loud cheers, and the Trucker triumphantly tossed the Coke can to the mat and stomped on it. "The Joy of Cola" played over the arena speakers, and the Trucker basked in the glow of thousands of adoring fans as Slaughter went to a commercial break. Machina rubbed his shoulder where the polar bear had scratched him with it's claws. It hurt some, but Machina wasn't that concerned. He sat on a bench and sighed. His Coke sponsorship was gone for sure. The Coca Cola representative who packed the polar bears back into their cages wouldn't even speak to him, and despite the mauling of their competitor's figurehead they were obviously not happy with him. With that disappointment under his belt he went in search of Rudo, and couldn't find him. Sid was in their dressing room, shouting into a telephone at his lame boyfriend David. Likewise Penny was strangely absent from the usual picketing that was going on outside. He was certain he would catch up with her sooner or later, after all they had a wedding to plan. Machina was hoping the wedding would happen before Ultra Violet's wedding. As far as he knew UV hadn't set a date, so Machina had a good change of beating it. Machina sighed again. He was about to lay back on the bench and catch a couple of winks before the show wrapped and everyone started heading off when a knock came at the door. "Come in..." The door opened, and the Mad Trucker entered. "Hey Ed." Machina waved, "Yeah what do you want Thomas? Come to gloat?" The Trucker sat, and offered a hand. "Nah, just came to say thanks. That was a great match. Listen we have our fights out there, but when I saw that little girl get mauled it occurred to me that these cola wars aren't really a big deal. Friends?" Machina looked at the outstretched hand carefully, and then smiled and shook it. "Yeah, you're right Thomas. It's just cola after all... it's not worth getting all angry about it. Sure I lost some money... but hey, easy come, easy go. Here... let's get some real drinks." The Mad Trucker stood. "Yeah... yeah, I'd like that." They walked over to the catering table and Machina reached for two cups. He handed one to the Trucker and picked up a bag of tea and tore it open. He steeped the tea into hot water, whistling as he did it. The Trucker watched this with a look of disgust on his face. "What in sam hill is this? You some kind of nancy boy?" Machina looked confused. "What? It's just tea... I always drink tea. Why... what do you drink?" The Trucker pointed to the canister of coffee. "Coffee! Coffee of course you little hoo-ha! You means to tell the Trucker that you're some kind of tea drinkin' nancy?" Machina glared at the Trucker and stabbed a finger into his chest. "Listen here you big lummock, tea is better for you, better tasting, and better all around. That black swill kills your sex drive, gives you bad breath, and isn't good for your heart!" The Trucker was aghast. "You... take... that... BACK!" He grabbed the canister of coffee and swung it at Machina, who ducked at the last second. Machina lunged at the Trucker, and fWo security quickly hit the scene to separate the two men. Machina screamed, "This isn't over fatty! This isn't over!" Trucker struggled against the security team holding him back. "I'll teach you what no good tea will do you no good nancy boy!" As the two men struggled against the men holding them back Slaughter went back to the ring... February 23, 2000 The WarfareScreen went to a live feed of a local Starbucks. The "Battle In A Coffee House" match was about to begin, and the Mad Trucker was already seated at the counter of the coffee shop, having himself his ninth cup of freshly brewed Starbucks coffee. The Trucker sighed with pleasure as he downed the coffee, marveling at the freshly roasted taste. The door to the coffee house opened, and Machina stood at the door with his arms behind his back, surveying the peaceful and yet very stylish scene before him. Machina smiled, and revealed what he had been holding behind his back. A cup of Lipton Tea. The patrons and the Trucker gasped as Machina raised an eyebrow, and then drank the tea down with one gulp. He then pointed to the woman wearing the stylish green apron behind the counter. "HA! That's right, Lipton tea... fresh, wholesome... and it doesn't cause heartburn or the jitters!" The Trucker jumped to his feet. "You... take... that... BACK!" He lunged at Machina as the match was officially underway. fWo officials locked the doors of the Starbucks, leaving both men trapped inside. Big right hands were thrown as the Trucker pounded the head of Machina, driving him backward into the stylish tables that had been set up throughout the restaurant. Machina struggled to get to his feet as the Mad Trucker spotted some copies of Doonsbury books that were for sale in Starbucks. He grabbed them and raised them over his head. Machina screamed, "No! Not that tired and unfunny cartoon strip!" The Mad Trucker flung the books at Machina and he yelped as he got out of the way in the nick of time. Back on his feet Machina ran to the other side of the shop looking for weapons. As he searched he yelled, "Coffee is just a conspiracy set forward by the Columbian cartel to try and hook the American populace by forcing them to drink a substance that leads to bad breath and shaky hands! With the US Army unable to aim a rocket correctly the invasion of the country will begin!" The Mad Trucker circled Machina and laughed at this claim. "Oh? What about your tea, nancy boy? Overpriced by the Asian economy, tea has long been a secret hippie source of hiding the wacky weed in with other ingredients and calling it an herbal supplement! It's just a facade for the pinko dictatorship that encourages marijuana usage!" Machina spat. "Well, DUH!" He spotted a row of expensive expresso machines and picked one up. "Hey Trucker... mocha this!" He tossed the equipment at the Trucker, striking the big man in the chest. The Trucker shrugged it off however, and advanced on Machina. "HA! Your fancy smancy coffee maker doesn't work on me... I'm not one of those foam drinkin' coffee nancys, I drink only the hard stuff... real coffee! The stuff you can find at truck stops that allow me to drive all night right through 'till mornin'!" Machina snapped his fingers. "Curses!" The Trucker grabbed Machina by the neck, and pulled him close. Machina struggled to get away, but the Trucker locked in a sleeper hold. "See, boy? If you drank a real drink you might be able to stay awake now... but your tea can't help you with this sleeper hold!" Machina struggled, but to no use. He started to fade, and the Trucker laughed. "Your tea can't save you know fancy pants!" Machina choked, and slowly he said, "True... but... one thing... you forgot... Trucker! You see... tea... satisfies your thirst... with only one cup... whereas your coffee... takes... more cups... and with more fluid..." The Trucker continued to lock in the hold, but seemed to be feeling uncomfortable. He shifted from foot to foot, and bit his bottom lip. Machina was nearly out, but continued speaking. "With... more fluid... and with... the way coffee... is harder to hold in... your bladder... you have to... go to the bathroom... more... often!" The Trucker was now showing serious signs of discomfort, and Machina summoned the last ounce of his strength to make running water noises with his mouth. The Trucker tried to hold on, but finally screamed and let go of the hold. He ran to the bathroom door, and found it locked. He pounded on it, and Machina cleared his throat behind him. The Trucker turned and saw what he feared most of all. Machina had the key to the bathroom. He smiled, and tossed it on the floor in front of him. The Trucker dove for it, and as his hands reached the key Machina quickly jumped on top of him and made a cover. The referee counted to three, and Machina leapt into the air the winner. Screaming he said, "You can take away our dignity... but you can't take away... OUR TEA!" Machina walked out of the wrecked Starbucks with the Trucker, both looking down the busy Nashville street and watching the cars go by. The Trucker spoke first. "Shucks... I don't know why I got all upset about coffee. I mean, a feller has a right to drink whatever he wants to drink... even if it is a nancy drink." Machina nodded. "Yeah, you're right Trucker. I can't believe I got so worked up about it. Something about Starbucks just makes me want to kill I guess. Anyway, no hard feelings?" Machina extended a hand. The Trucker grabbed it, pumped it, and released. "No hard feelings littl' buddy. Say... you want to see a movie?" The Trucker pointed across the street to where a movie theatre stood. Machina looked at it and nodded. "You know, why not? There isn't any big reason to go back to the arena right now... I'm sure that Penny can take care of herself and nothing bad will happen. What do you want to see?" The Trucker scanned the movie titles on the sign out in front. "I don't know... not very much sounds good..." Machina looked at the titles with a frown on his face, and then suddenly broke out into a smile. "There! The new James Bond movie... let's go to see that!" The Trucker grunted in disgust. "Ugh... no thanks. The only real Bond is Shawn Connery... this new nancy is all hype and no delivery." Machina gasped. "What? I'll have you know that Pierce Brosnon is the best James Bond there has ever been! You just shut your mouth about your stupid old fart Connery. He's too fat and too stupid to be a real Bond... besides, what kind of British secret agent has a Scottish accent?" The Trucker shoved Machina and glared at him. "Oh right, like some dumb American will ever fill the shoes of Connery... Pierce Brosnon should be off somewhere doing hoo-ha pornos and not soiling the legacy that Shawn Connery left behind!" Machina gritted his teeth. "Thems fightin' words big man!" The Trucker raised his fists. "Bring it on then... fWo Trapped... you and me... James Bond Match!" Machina spat. "I'll see you there tubby!" The second referee went to the outside and retrieved the World Title. He brought it into the ring and raised Perfect' hand, but the first referee disagreed and took the World Title from him and gave it to Primetime, before raising his hand. Both men were still out of it and moving slowly on the mat, confused at what had happened. The referees continued to disagree with one another before Chris Finn & James Armstrong came out and conferred with both referees. Primetime and Perfect slowly made it to their feet, and the referees informed Finn & Armstrong what had happened and why each man thought they were correct in making the call. Finn & Armstrong privately talked after hearing each referee out, before coming to a decision. Finn & Armstrong decided that each referee were neither right or wrong in making the call, but since it wouldn't be fair to either man to declare one of them champion since both men had the other pinned, they would come to another decision. They decided that with the attention on Body Count and it being such a huge event for the fWo, the title would be held up and the winner of Body Count would become the new fWo World Champion. The ring announcer announced this to the crowd, who reacted with some negativity towards it but they realized this made the main event of the next pay-per-view that much more important. Primetime & Perfect both seemed furious at the decision, but walked away from the ring knowing they would each have their opportunity come March 25. From there, Slaughter went off the air. Sunday Slaughter - May 21, 2000 - The Future Foundation is forme The twenty-five thousand plus fans inside the Skydome in Toronto, Canada anxiously stood on their feet as the first Slaughter in four weeks took to the air. Sirens screeched throughout the arena, while fireworks were released from the stage. Suddenly, "Juke Joint Jezebel" began to play, and the flag of France appeared on the SlaughterScreen. However, a big circle with a slash through it appeared, and "Juke Joint Jezebel" stopped. Instead, "Last Resort" by Papa Roach began to play, and out walked Primetime, Courage & presumably the Frenchie; but upon closer inspection one could guess that it was Lance Star dressed in his Future Frenchie gear. The fans sat speechless as the trio of arrogant young men entered the ring; Primetime with his fWo Internet Title draped over his shoulder, Courage with his fWo International Title wrapped around his waist, and Star with his newly won fWo Cruiserweight Title over his shoulder. Star handed his belt to Courage, who held it while Star took off his beret, the mask, and his Future Frenchie t-shirt, before tossing it all to the ground. Primetime then received a microphone and began to speak. "Pierre... Pierre... Pierre. You know, I've never felt this good in my entire life. Why? The French Foundation is dead. Pierre, you held me back for two years. You knew two years ago when you came to Mitch and I that our careers were a disappointment up to that point; but you knew that given the right roles our careers would flourish. And that they did. So you put together this so-called French Foundation... and even have the audacity to get Mitch and I to become citizens of France! My ancestors aren't French, they're Polish for crying out loud! Mitch and I knew it was bad... but we decided, what the hell. He had been the Dude, a surfer for Christ's sake, and I had been the leader of a group that couldn't get over if our lives depended on it in the Primetime Players. So, surely being French couldn't be worse than that, right? Wrong. Mitch & I sit in that putrid group... and we became a tag team. And we won the belts right off the bat. Surely, the suits started to take notice... and we then drifted into singles competition for awhile. And while we didn't exactly win any singles titles, we were damn successful. Until last Summer, and that horrible angle where we had to pretend we were truck drivers. TRUCK DRIVERS! I'm not a truck driver, I'm Primetime, and I'm the reason each and every single one of the fans is here tonight. Mitch knew it was ridiculous, but I stuck around... went onto win Meltdown, and you were jealous... you were jealous. I end up winning the Cruiserweight Title, then the Women's Title and decide to give it to you out of the kindness of my heart. So, then I go on and defeat Scott Slugger later in the year for the fWo World Title. Did you congratulate me? No! You expected a freaking title shot, and so I gave you one. And who came out on top? ME. And then Invasion! rolls around... I go from facing The Boston Strangler at Twilight, to wrestling Matt Douglas in the opening match in my supposed "hometown"; while you're in the main event against Mr. No-Charisma himself, The Black Panther. The next night, you, I and Courage meet in a three-way dance. Who wins? ME. And then finally you do something good for me... you have the fWo World Title, and you recognize that last December after I lost the title in a match I wasn't even pinned in, that I never got a rematch. So, you give me one at Trapped... and who comes out on top? ME! Do you get where I'm going with this, Pierre? I'm far superior than you, and you saw that... and you decided to attempt to hold me back and you damn nearly did. Frustration Builds For Scott Collins Collins brings Bear up again, and drops him down with a brainbuster. Collins heads to the corner and mounts the second turnbuckle, before leaping off and driving the elbow into the throat of Bear. Collins covers, but Bear again shows his resiliency by kicking out before the three count is tolled. Collins brings Bear up once again and attempts to take him over with a double-underhook suplex, but Bear fights desperately out of it and backdrops Collins over his head. In the process, he seem to get a second wind. As Collins gets up, Bear hiptosses him up and over, followed by leaping into the air with a beautiful dropkick that strikes Collins flush in the face. Collins makes his way to his feet again and is met by a superkick which knocks him against the ropes. Bear then charges at Collins, taking both men over with a Cactus clothesline. Both men crash to the outside, and after a moment, Bear reaches his feet again and rolls Collins in. He brings Collins up and attempts the Whiplash, but Collins slides down his shoulders and hooks his head on the way down for the Death Penalty. Bear holds his ground however and shoves Collins off into the ropes. He catches Collins coming off, lifts him into a fireman's carry, and wastes no time in hitting the Whiplash on his second attempt. Bear makes the cover, and the ref counts one... two... three, and Bear retains his International Title. The referee goes to the outside and receives Bear' strap, before bringing it back in the ring and handing it to Bear. Bear holds it for the crowd to see, which draws a large cheer. Bear straps it around his waist and exits the ring, and begins walking backstage. As he does however, Collins asks for a microphone and is given one... "Hey, Bear. Turn around. I'm not done yet. I'll give you your due. You beat me. But I want one more chance. One more chance to prove to you, the rest of the world, and most importantly myself that I have what it takes. I want to prove that I still belong around here. I've been the Internet Champion, I've been a Tag Team Champion, I've held that very belt that is around your waist right now, I've been a Hardcore Champion... and I've done what I'm sure you'll do someday Bear, and I've held the most prized posession this industry of ours has to offer: The fWo World Title. I've held it not just once, but twice. And I was the flagbearer for this company for many months, so I know I still have what it takes. I just want to prove it to myself. So, here's what I'm proposing to you. I want another match at Critical Condition... between you and I. Your title doesn't have to be on the line, because this isn't about a title to me right now. Don't worry about that. But I just request one more match between you and myself. And if I don't win... if my arm isn't raised at the end of that night, then I shouldn't be in the ring. And that's not an offense to you, because you'll be able to brag that you were the one who retired the great Scott Collins. So, what do you say, Bear? One on one at Critical Condition. If I can't win... I'll leave the fWo." Collins put his hands near his hips as he waited for a response from the International Champion. Bear considered the offer for several minutes before shaking his head, and pointing at Collins, telling him he had a deal. Collins smiled and held a fist in the air, before telling Bear that victory would be his come June 24th. Roberts: Baby! fWo World Title Match - Primetime vs. Ric Chronos Slaughter returned to the ring for the main event of the evening, a rematch from Fallout just twenty four hours ago in the semi-finals of the Wrestler of the Year Tournament. The title wasn't on the line for that match, but tonight, it was. "Christian Says" by Tones On Tail began to play, and out walked the 2000 Wrestler of the Year. The fans inside the Continental Airlines Arena stood to their feet, and began to cheer wildly at the appearance of the 'Man Of The Hour'. Chronos stepped out onto the stage, before making his journey down the ramp and into the ring. Chronos began doing some warm-up exercises, before his music faded. It was replaced by 'Snap' by Slipknot, which signalled the entrance of the reigning fWo World Heavyweight Champion. Primetime. Chronos looked to the stage, and saw the man who nearly ended his career this past August 17th. With fire in his ways, Chronos licked his lips. Perhaps he was clearing something off of them that was disturbing him, or perhaps he was literally biting at the chomps to get ahold of his number one enemy. Either way, we would find out momentarily. Primetime walked down the ramp, taunting the fans as he did. He looked in the ring and saw Chronos, and saw a man who was hellbent on winning the title tonight. But in the mind of Primetime, it wasn't going to happen. We would all soon find out. Primetime made his way down to ringside, and continued to talk trash with some fans. With his attention momentarily distracted, Chronos saw his opportunity. He sprung to the top rope immediately, and flew off with a corkscrew elbow to the face of the champion. Both men crashed to the floor. Primetime again found himself in a situation where the match began outside the ring for the second consecutive night. Chronos stood to his feet and stared at the sea of fans, and knew his time had come. He grabbed Primetime by the head and brought him up, slamming him head first into the ring, and then the guardrail. He then backed him up against a ringpost, and chopped him across the chest hard. Sounds of flesh against flesh echoed throughout the arena. Chronos then Irish Whipped him towards the guardrail, but Primetime reversed it. Chronos however reversed it again, but instead, hip tossed him over the guardrail. Fortunately, there were no fans around, and instead Primetime smacked the pavement back first. Chronos then grabbed a nearby steel chair. Since the match hadn't officially started, he wouldn't be disqualified. Chronos came back over to where Primetime was located, and as he climbed up off the cement floor, Chronos swung downwards, and cracked the chair across his head. He connected. Chronos then climbed over the guardrail, and instructed the fans to back away from the action. They complied, and Chronos winded up for another chair shot. Instead, Primetime ducked, and Chronos connected this time with a security guard. The guard fell to the ground in pain. Chronos attempted to apologize, but this gave Primetime an opportunity. Primetime grabbed him from behind with a waistlock. Chronos knew what was coming, and had to avoid it. Somehow. Someway. The Backdrop Driver. Had Primetime hit that move, the match would have never started. Instead, Chronos escaped with a go-behind, and once he was there, he grabbed Primetime by the head, and pulled him in. Lowfall. Chronos looked down at Primetime, and told him that was for "John Skyler". Chronos then brought Primetime up and sent him flying over the guardrail. Chronos followed. Primetime got up, and Chronos rolled him underneath the bottom rope into the ring. Chronos again followed. With both men in the ring, the referee signalled for the bell. The match was now officially underway. Chronos immediately went for a cover. One. Two. Primetime got his foot on the bottom rope. Not discouraged, Chronos brought Primetime up once again, and sent him to the ropes. Primetime returned, and found himself falling asleep shortly thereafter. The Hourglass. Chronos had the sleeperhold locked in, but not enough. Primetime back suplexed out of it to break the hold, and then took a moment to recover from all that he had endured so far in the match. That break didn't last long. Chronos was ready to rock and roll, and Primetime knew he had to do something to stifle his momentum. Primetime brought Chronos up and sent him to the ropes. Chronos returned and leapfrogged the ducking Primetime. When he returned, Primetime grabbed him and went for a hip toss. It was blocked, and then countered into a hip toss. That was also blocked, and countered into a hip toss. Finally, with both men near the ropes, Primetime hip tossed Chronos to the outside. But Chronos held on, and landed on the ring apron. Chronos then dashed over and climbed up the turnbuckles. He flew off with a flying bodypress, but the momentum caused Primetime to end up on top of him for the second pinfall attempt. One. Two. Kickout. Both men jumped to their feet, and Chronos brought Primetime off his feet with a quick legsweep. Chronos then followed it up with a rolling pinfall attempt. One. Two. A move that won him the Wrestler of the Year tournament... from the top rope. The Figure Four DDT. He had him locked in position, but Primetime blocked, escaped, and fought free with a lowblow. Ric brought Primetime up and rolled him into the ring. For good measure, he hit a Michinoku Driver for the pin. One. Two. But Primetime kicked out. Seeing as how it was going to take a big time move to finish the champion off, Chronos hooked him... and ran towards the corner. Chronosphere. But Primetime escaped and pushed Chronos out of it in mid-air. Chronos collided with the referee, who unfortunately happened to be standing in the way. But there was no referee. Seeing as how the referee was out, Primetime sought to finish things off through devious means. Time elapsed, but another referee charged down to ringside. He climbed in, and counted. One. Two. Thre - no. Chronos somehow, someway kicked out. The arena erupted in cheers. This time, Chronos got a shoulder up. Primetime was more than angry, he was more than pissed, he was furious. The reigning champion stood to his feet, and went to the top rope. There, he flew off with a legdrop, perhaps to mock his hated enemy the Flying Frenchie, who was surely following the action closely in the back. Having connected, Primetime thought that wasn't enough. Instead, he pushed the referee aside, and sent Chronos' limp body flying over the top rope to the floor. Primetime then methodically stalked him outside the ring. As the fans watched intently on what Primetime was going to do next, Primetime decided to take things to a new level. He slowly climbed to the top rope. He looked out at the fans, and without hesitation - without any trepidation, he flew off. Guilotine Legdrop through the table. Jeremiah Frankton & Stevie Roberts ran for their lives. Pieces of the table were scattered throughout the Continental Airlines Arena. Monitors were out. The arena was in awe. They did not think Primetime would do something like that... but they soon found out they were wrong. Time passed, and finally, Primetime got to his feet first. Two. Two and 99 ?/100ths. Chronos got his foot on the rope... but just barely. Primetime was furious. He jumped to his feet, and began mouthing off to the referee. From behind, Chronos grabbed him, and took him down with a rolling suplex. He brought him back up, and hit another. He brought him up for a third time, but Primetime escaped with a go-behind. Chronos had put up a tremendous fight, but could not escape defeat. Primetime stood in the ring victorious, although just barely. The referee recovered the fWo World Title for him, and Primetime realized just how lucky he was to win this match. It looked as if Primetime knew that he was quite fortunate... and might've considered congratulating Chronos on the match if he wasn't such an evil bastard. No matter. Before he got the chance; the lights went out... ... and "Juke Joint Jezebel" by KMFDM began to play. Out walked The Flying Frenchie. Primetime stood in the ring with his mouth agape. Hoping that Frenchie wasn't out to start anything physically, Primetime locked his eyes on the man he hated more than any other human on this planet. Frenchie stopped on the stage, and waited for his music and the large amount of cheers to die down. Finally, it did. And Frenchie had something to say. "Your career... my career... our careers... on ze line at Twilight? Is zat really what you want, Brian? If zat is what you want, I will be more zan happy to end your career in a few weeks. Under a few ot'er stipulations. First of all, the match must end wit' a pinfall or a submission. Secondly, ze match can have no interference whatsoever. Any interference will result in indefinite suspension to ze offenders, even if zey are interfering on my behalf. If you can agree to zose rules... zen I will see you at Twilight. Are we in agreement, Brian?" Primetime dropped his title to the mat, and looked down at it. Sweat dripped off his forehead, as referees helped the bloody Ric Chronos from the ring. A look of anger came over the face of Primetime, and he looked out at the crowd. He then looked at Frenchie... and at that moment, realized he could not turn back on his answer. But that was fine with him. This was one situation he had to take care of himself. With his eyes focused on the face of what was once a close friend, Primetime shook his head yes several times to make sure there was no confusion as to what his "final answer" was. Frenchie beamed, before he told Primetime in French that he was going to receive an early ass kicking. Frenchie slowly began to walk towards the ring, and Primetime braced himself. As Frenchie reached ringside, it appeared all hell would break loose between two men who despised each other with every fiber in their body. Instead, however, a face emerged from the crowd. "Kodiak" Vic Creed. From behind, Creed spun the World Champion around, and just as he had done to Ross last night at Fallout and earlier tonight, nailed Primetime with a Hangman's DDT. Creed then stood over Primetime with a smirk, before looking out at Frenchie, and asking him if he wanted some too. Before Frenchie could respond... Slaughter faded to black. Dec-00 Austin Davis Wants To Face The fWo World Champion At Countdown 1 December 2000 for the fans Wrestling organization kicked off in Washington, DC at the MCI Center. Inside the arena, fWo Commentators Jeremiah Frankton & Stevie Roberts walked down the entrance ramp to some cheers, although it should be noted that Roberts' cheers were much louder than Frankton's. The fans welcomed them to ringside, and they took their seats in preparation to call tonight's action. Inside the ring, fWo Ring Announcer Matt Panzer told the fans that we would go live in twenty seconds. The fans cheered loudly, and finally after the twenty seconds had passed - fireworks began shooting off on the stage, and music blared loudly throughout the arena. The fans rose to their feet and cheered, as finally the show that had been sold out since mid-October had taken to the air. With a surprise, it seemed. "Beautiful People" began blaring throughout the MCI Center, and a red strobe light flickered on and off which of course was the entrance for one of the most hated - if not the most hated - men in fWo history. Austin Davis. Dead. The fans began booing immediately at his mere presence. They had no reason to like him - back in January, he attempted to kill the fWo. In April, he became the official owner of the fWo, and shut it down for three weeks. In August, he began playing a series of mind games with The Boston Strangler, and it is safe to say that Davis is a big reason for the latest change in attitude of the Strangler. Nevertheless, Davis looked at the fans, and snarled. He didn't care what the fans thought. Why should he? He was a three time fWo World Champion without the help of the fans. He didn't need them, and he certainly didn't want them to be in his corner. Davis now stood in the ring with thousands of hostile, angry fans around him - booing loudly. Without hesitation, Davis gave them the middle finger, before reaching into his black trenchcoat and pulling out a microphone. As his music came to a halt, and the arena went back to it's usual status, Davis began to let everyone know just exactly why he was here. "For those of you who don't quite remember... earlier this year, before CyberSlam... back in January, I was in control. I don't like to admit defeat... but The Boston Strangler took the power I had away from me... twice. When I was in control in the beginning, it was from a different standpoint. Not an ownership standpoint... but a booking slash commissioner type standpoint. If you remember... with that power... I ensured that I and Scott Slugger - who was just another person I used to get what I wanted - would receive title shots if we lost our said power... at any point in the future. When we decided... we would each receive a shot. We both knew that after what we had done to this company, there was no way that Chris Finn & James Armstrong would give me or him a title shot. That changed for Scott, obviously... as he kissed a little ass and got back in their good graces. But now he's paying for it... injured... at the hands of The Boston Strangler. Back to my point... and just to back all of this up, if you would all take a look at the SlaughterScreen... I'll have those fWo technical geeks play the evidence of this." Davis stopped talking, and pointed everyone's attention to the SlaughterScreen, where a videotape of the 01.23.00 Slaughter played, proving that what Davis was saying was true. "Oh, one last thing, should the Boston Strangler do the impossible and defeat me here tonight, and let's face it, the chances of that happening aren't very good... should this be our last night of power, well... we're going to take out an insurance policy for ourselves. That's right. At some point in the future, whenever WE decide, we both will receive a shot at the fWo World Title. And at any point in the future, we may put this into affect... whether you like it or not." The videotape stopped, and Davis resumed speaking. "Well, I've decided that the time is right... not tonight... but at the final televised show of this year - Countdown To Oblivion 1 - I will be facing whomever the fWo World Champion is. Be it Primetime... be it The Flying Frenchie... be it Mike Bear... be it anyone else, they will face me. And just as Jim Dudley did... just as Stevie Roberts did... just as the entire fWo did... they will suffer extreme pain and punishment... they will be crucified. The countdown has just begun. Prepare for oblivion. Trust me." Davis outstretched his arms in a crucifixion style pose... and the fans again booed him as they had done earlier. This time, Davis completely ignored all of it, and exited the ring. He was then mobbed by the crowd, as he decided to officially exit near the fans. Slaughter had officially began with an unexpected visitor... and while the shock was still setting in, Slaughter took to it's first commercial break. Courage Must Leave The fWo Star went to Irish Whip Primetime to the ropes, but Primetime reversed it. The Hollywood native returned and somehow rolled Primetime up into a single legged Boston Crab. Primetime writhed in pain, and tried to escape, but Star locked in more pressure. It appeared Star had Primetime just where he wanted him. There was no escaping this move. Primetime appeared to be in an abundance of agony as Star continued to apply the hold with as much pressure as he could. Primetime cried for help, but there was no help. Finally, Primetime realized that he was going to have to do this alone - just as he would have to face The Flying Frenchie alone on December 16th at Twilight. Primetime began to crawl towards the ropes in attempt to break the hold. Moments passed, and after a great struggle - Primetime did reach the ropes. The referee gave Star until the count of five to break the hold. Star released it at four, figuring that had been enough to put Primetime away. Star decided to go for a cover on Courage since Courage was still seemingly out of it. One. Two. No. Somehow, Courage kicked out at two and a half, but it must've been since so much time had passed since Lance laid his former tag team partner out with the top rope modified Urange suplex. Disgruntled, Lance went back to work on Primetime, and whipped him to the ropes. Lance ducked for a backdrop, but Primetime telegraphed it. Unlike earlier, this time Primetime did go for his old finisher. Primetime Bomb. He laid the same man who had defeated him on Halloween of last year out, but decided that he wasn't going to just pin Star and force him to leave the fWo, he was going to permanently end his career, just as he had promised days ago. Primetime got in a location where he would be behind Lance when Lance got to his feet. Lance finally did, and found himself grasped in the arms of Primetime in a waistlock. We all knew what was coming. Lance knew what was coming. But before he could attempt to escape, it appeared as if Lance would fall victim to the same move that ended the careers of Havok & Fallen, and put both Ric Chronos & Wolf Fang on the shelf for a lengthy duration of time. The Backdrop Driver. Suddenly, the lights went out. The crowd went silent as they heard something happen in the ring. Fans held their lighters up in an attempt (foolishly) to see what was going on in the ring. Fans wondered if Primetime had succeeded. They would soon find out. A hush fell over the crowd, but not before some music began playing, and one man kept his vow that he would not let Primetime end his best friend' career. "If you buyyy... what the Sellout... is selling!" was heard for the second straight week. And like the previous week, fans rose to their feet and ovated. Cheering wildly, the lights came back on, and "Mr. Sellout" Chris Universal was on the entrance ramp, looking to come down and kick Primetime' ass. But... something prevented him from doing so. A large group of fWo security surrounded him, and kept him from coming towards the ring. With the lights back on, this also revealed that Lance was laid out in the ring, but was moving slightly. Nobody knew if Primetime had succeeded in executing the feared backdrop driver, except Primetime & Lance themselves. It could be assumed that he did, since Lance was out of it on the mat, and Primetime was standing up. Primetime dared Universal to come to the ring so they could get it on, when he heard a commotion from behind him. For the third time in the match, Courage had gone for the asai moonsault. Unlike the first time he attempted the move on Lance, this time he hit it - the referee positioned himself as Courage covered the same man who once donned some Frenchie-like attire and called himself 'The Future Frenchie'. One. Two. No. Primetime broke up the hold by pulling Courage off of Star, and bringing him up. He hooked him for what appeared to be a vertical suplex, but instead, Primetime decided to go for the kill. Slingshot Michinoku Driver. Snapshot. It was over. One. Two. Three. And as a result of the pre-agreed to match stipulations, Courage was forced to leave the fWo. Nobody was sure if that meant his career was over, or when we would see Courage again, but it was safe to say that it would be a long, long time from now when that day would come. Primetime stood to his feet and celebrated, as he retained the most coveted prize in our game, the fans Wrestling organization Heavyweight Championship. Suddenly, people spotted the Flying Frenchie coming down the through crowd, and began to cheer. At the same time, Universal escaped the grasp of the fWo security members, and began heading towards the ring. Primetime saw Universal coming, but looked around and saw Frenchie hopping over the guardrail, wielding a baseball bat. Frenchie quickly entered the ring, and at the same time, Universal entered from the other side. Frenchie moved towards Primetime and swung, but Primetime ducked. Instead, The Sellout was struck in the chest with the Lousiville Slugger, and he fell to the mat. Frenchie turned and again swung at Primetime, only this time he didn't miss, and Primetime fell to the mat in a heap. The fans cheered wildly, and Frenchie decided to deal out some more punishment to the same man he would be facing at Twilight in a match that would have both of their careers on the line. Frenchie went to the top rope, and prepared to fly. Suddenly, from seemingly out of nowhere, The Boston Strangler appeared, and shoved Frenchie off the top rope. Frenchie landed in the center of the ring, and the fans booed loudly, not sure what else to think about the actions of a man they used to love, but now loved to hate. The Strangler entered the ring, and applied the Boston Massacre. The Strangler had a blank expression on his face as he 'caused Frenchie to cry out in pain... but it was all the Strangler knew. It was his livelyhood. Universal stood to his feet and saw Frenchie in the hold, and didn't seem to care as Universal didn't know if Frenchie intended to nail him with the baseball bat. Sure, it could be assumed that Frenchie was going after Primetime... but even if Frenchie told Universal this, Universal likely wouldn't believe him. In the past, Frenchie had been known to lie once in awhile, afterall. Universal instead looked for Star, who was struggling to get up. Universal walked towards his best friend and attempted to help him up, but Star shoved him away. Shocked, Universal tried to talk to Star, but Star ignored him and exited the ring. Universal watched him walk away and tried to look for answers... tried to see if Star would look back, but Star never did. December 10th Slaughter Highlights The fWo's only television show returned from a commercial break to find Megan Dawn already standing in the ring. It seems as if the crowd remembers her despite the fact she hasn't been on television in quite some time. We catch the trail end of a favorable reaction to her, before Dawn addresses the crowd. "I'm here tonight to do a favor" Dawn says, brushing her blondish/brown hair to hair to the side, revealing a chest full of ample cleveage, which is covered in spots by her somewhat revealing outfit. "A favor for Chris Universal. I don't know what the deal with Lance Star is. But Lance... you need to realize that Chris is your friend, and last Sunday, when he successfully prevented you from becoming permanently disabled at the hands of Primetime, he was doing you a favor. We both know and realize that you're a grown man and you can take care of yourself, but sometimes, everyone needs a little help from their friends. And Chris is your friend - not your enemy. Primetime - Primetime is the enemy. Not me, not Chris... and not these fans here in Raleigh, North Carolina!" Longtime fWo fans noted that Universal had a favorable working relationship with the wwf's Mick Foley, who was an acquaintance of Travis Beaven and worked for Beaven while he was the owner of the fWo - and obviously, Dawn herself had learned something from Foley as well: How to draw a cheap pop from the crowd. "Or anyone else, for that matter. Lance... would you please come out here, right now, so we can talk about this? Please? If you won't listen to Chris - at least... listen to me?" The crowd looked immediately to the stage as 'My Generation' by Limp Bizkit began to play, which brought out 'The Future' Lance Star. Star looked at the crowd who responded somewhat negatively to his presence... but Star ignored it, as evident by the smug expression on his face. Star reached the ring and climbed in apprehensively, as he was obviously unsure about Dawn. Dawn & Star were friends as well, perhaps because they shared a mutual friend in the case of Universal. But as we learned this previous week, it had been sometime since Star & Dawn had spoken. Star stood across from Dawn, as Dawn thanked him for coming down. Star seemed unfazed by what she said, and Dawn struggled to recover as to not be embarassed by Lance' lack of response. "Anyways... Lance, Chris is your friend. Chris is not trying to be your big brother - he never was. Friends help friends, that's just the way it is. Lance, Chris was just trying to help you, much like he did last year when you wear nearly paralyzed. Don't you remember that? Either way, Chris succeeded. You weren't seriously injured by Primetime last Sunday, were you? And you couldn't even thank him for that. But that's not the important issue - Lance, Chris does not want to fight you. Can't you see that? He wants to be your friend. He's sorry about not calling you, but can't you realize that his father was dying? Can't you realize that he was on his death bed, and Chris had to take care of his father?" Lance still seemed unfazed by what Megan Dawn was saying, and continued to have a lifeless expression on his face. Dawn continued. "Lance, please... talk to Chris, straight this matter out. You both need to talk this situation out, and apologize, and bury any hurt feelings between the two of you. Please. I care about you both." Dawn handed Lance the microphone, and without hesitation, Lance began talking. Coldly, he looked Dawn right in the eyes. "Are you done yet? Good. Because I'm tired of you rambling. Let me ask you something, Megan. Don't you realize that Chris Universal is, has been, and always will be about himself? When you wanted to spend time with him - what was he out doing? Wrestling. His work life engulfed him. But you were fortunate in that you could travel on the road with him, and even then, he didn't pay attention to you. He went to fancy restaraunts, he went to lavish hotels, he slept around with other women..." The crowd "oohed" at this, but it didn't seem as if they believed Lance. "He treated people like crap. And all the while they bought his sincerity, they bought that he was trying to help me, that he cared about me. If he cared about me, why did he not pick up that phone after he left the fWo back in March?" Dawn seemed ready to answer, but Lance interrupted her. "I'm not finished yet." The crowd again "oohed". "Now, something tells me, that just like he does, YOU need to stay out of my business. Am I clear here, Megan?" It appeared as if Dawn was taken aback by Lance' heartless attitude, but nodded slightly. "Good. Now if I may be serious for a moment - you look like a slut. How 'bout having that rich boy shell out some dough and arrange for you to have a makeover?" The crowd hissed and booed at this, as Star continued to stare at Dawn coldly. Dawn had enough of his attitude, and slapped him. Lance held his face, before grabbing a hold of Dawn' shirt. "You tell Chris that come next Saturday, I own him." Lance pushed Dawn backwards and she stumbled to the mat. The crowd booed again, and Lance just laughed off the comments, staring down at the fallen Dawn with a callous snarl.
