Frankton: An unbelievable win for Lance Star, in an unbelievable match that could've gone either way. Roberts: Big win for Lance, and surely, you'd have to put him back in that upper echelon that he so desires with a couple of more wins like this, baby. Frankton: I'd have to concur. Lance now disappears to the back, while Universal getting up in the ring, in a state of disbelief. fWo Twilight - Career Match, fWo World Title On The Line - Prime Primetime: Frenchie, it's almost time. It's been 10 months, Frenchie, since we've met one on one in the ring. Who won that last match, Frenchie? I did, in the cage, at Trapped. Who won on a Warfare last November between us, Frenchie? I did. Who pinned you two months ago at Halloween Hell, Frenchie? I did. Primetime: This is the last time, Frenchie. Primetime and the Flying Frenchie will meet for the absolute last time, and this may be the absolute last time that one of us ever steps into an fWo ring. It's not about my hatred for you, your hatred for me. Primetime: It's not about the World Title. MY World Title, Frenchie. It's about my career. It's about your career. Everything that either of us have ever accomplished inside of the squared circle, none of that matters anymore, because after one of us is pinned in the middle of that ring, those accomplishments mean jack-shit. Primetime: Frenchie...I'd be lying if I said I wasn't scared. I know you are too, but there's one thing that we can do. Wrestle. One of us is gone, one of us stays. One of us goes home empty handed, one of us goes home World Champion. I may be scared, but Frenchie, I do like my chances. Do you like yours? Frenchie: It comes down to zis, zen. Primetime fWo World Title Match - Primetime vs. Ric Chronos "Christian Says" by Tones on Tail brought out the challenger for tonight's big fWo World Title Match. The 2000 Wrestler Of The Year and former fWo Internet Champion Ric Chronos stepped out onto the stage, and was greeted with an ovation of cheers from the packed house of twenty one thousand fans. Chronos acknowledged some of the fans with high fives as he walked down the entrance ramp, looking quite ready for action. He rolled into the ring and immediately began running back and forth, before stopping and jumping a few times, and then using the ropes to stretch some muscles. The cheers quieted down, before they turned into boos. "Snap" by Slipknot replaced the sounds of Tones On Tail, and out walked out the fans Wrestling organization Heavyweight Champion, Primetime. The pompous, arrogant twenty two year old walked out onto the stage. He raised his arms in the air, and then brought the bottle of water he held in his hand down to his mouth, before he took a swig. The champion then doused his medium length hair with some of the water, before tossing the empty bottle off the stage. Primetime began walking down the ramp, and Chronos couldn't take the mere sight of him anymore. As was the case the last time these two men wrestled the night after fWo Fallout back on 11.19.00, Chronos departed the ring. Not with a corkscrew elbow like last time, this time he went to the floor with a baseball slide. Primetime prepared for him and unstrapped the fWo World Title off his waist, and tossed it to the side. Chronos went towards Primetime, and it was on. The two men exchanged glancing blows at first, before the fistfight developed into an all out slugfest. Back and forth the two men went, before Primetime put a halt to the fisticuffs with a knee to the stomach. He then whipped the challenger backfirst into the guardrail, and then searched for a weapon to introduce to Chronos. The fWo World Title. He clutched it in both hands, before charging at the 'Man Of The Hour' and preparing to lay waste to him. Chronos was ready for it, and instead backdropped the champion into the crowd. Primetime landed on a group of fans, and you could hear Jeremiah Frankton (who was doing solo play-by-play tonight due to the departure of Stevie Roberts) remind everyone at home that the fWo was not responsible for damage incurred on behalf of the rampaging talent. Chronos then turned and with his mouth open, saw Primetime getting up. But he also saw beyond that. He saw a man who damn near attempted to ruin his chances of doing the one thing he loved - wrestling. Months of frustration came out with Chronos' next move. Chronos charged and went flying over the guardrail with a shoulderblock. Both men landed on the exposed concrete with a sickening thud. The referee recovered the World Title, and handed it back to a ringside attendant, who placed it on the timekeepers table. Chronos & Primetime laid on the floor just a minute and a half into their battle, already exhausted. The referee then climbed into the sea of fans, joining the two combatants, as they struggled to recover from what had just happened. Primetime was backtowards the guardrail, and he pulled himself up. Chronos tried to get up on his own two feet, but fell back down. A lot was riding on this match. The emotion driving these two men was off the charts. Primetime turned and saw Chronos getting up. The champion walked up from behind and spun Chronos around. PT swung, but Chronos blocked, and nailed Primetime with a solid shot to the stomach. He then unloaded with furious jabs, nine of them to be exact. On the tenth one, he grabbed the dazed Primetime and then turned to the crowd for a reaction. They responded overwhelmingly with cheers. With a look of revenge in his eyes, Chronos hauled off and nailed Primetime, who fell backwards to the floor. The referee told Chronos to take the action to the ring - afterall, Chronos was involved in a match last night that saw little involvement in the ring, if any. Primetime was fresh off a match that saw it's fair share of action outside of the ring as well. With that in mind, Chronos decided to ignore the referee's wishes. Chronos politely asked a fan for his chair. The fans moved aside, as Chronos grabbed it. A groggy Primetime came to and stood to his feet. He turned around and Chronos swung with the chair, but the referee was their to grab it from behind. The fans began booing, and Chronos asked him what the hell he was doing. With his attention distracted, Primetime charged at Chronos and took him down with a tackle. Both men found themselves on the concrete once again. The referee gave the chair back to the fan, who used it to sit down. Primetime & Chronos again struggled to their feet, and Primetime grabbed Chronos by the head, before slamming him head first into the guardrail. Primetime then dumped him over back onto the safe side of things, before joining him. Primetime then looked for a weapon of his own to use - and found the steel steps. He removed the top portion and walked towards Chronos, preparing to inflict major damage. The referee distracted his attention momentarily, telling him to put the weapon down. This allowed Chronos to get up - and the Cincinatti native took Primetime down with a dropkick to the steps. Primetime fell, and the steps fell on him. Thinking quickly, Chronos then decided to inflict some damage on Primetime with the steps, who were on PT's chest. He charged and stepped on the steps, springing himself up to the ring apron. Chronos then strategically waited for Primetime to get up - finally, the champion did, after moving the steps off of him first. PT groggily got to a position where he was bent over, and the next thing Chronos did was shocking. Chronos walked over and simply leaped off the ring apron with a legdrop, taking Primetime down again with a variation of the "rocker dropper". The fans "oohed" in response, not sure what to think. Chronos pulled himself up with aide of the ring apron, before rolling into the ring. The referee and Chronos exchanged words once in there, as Chronos got the crowd into the match. Primetime remained lying on the floor in a daze. A minute after, Primetime stood to his feet, just barely. He cracked his neck with his hands, before he rolled into the ring. Chronos walked over and grabbed him by the arm, sending him to the opposite side of the ring with an Irish Whip. Primetime reversed it, and when Chronos returned he went for a spinning heel kick - but Primetime caught him in mid-air and planted him into the mat with reckless abandon. With both men now in the ring, the referee signalled for the bell, then went to the mat to count the ensuing pinfall. One. Two. No. Chronos got a shoulder up, and now the match slowed down a bit as Primetime was still reeling from the innovative move Chronos executed on him outside the ring moments ago. Primetime stood to his feet and looked down at Chronos with disdain, before unloading some stomps to his back and neck area. Chronos attempted to fight it off by returning with punches, but couldn't. Instead he tried to get up, which would prevent Primetime from kicking him. He backed to the ropes and Primetime dealt him a barrage of reverse knife edge chops. The Atlanta crowd responded with "Whoo!" every time Primetime' hand smacked the chest of the challenger. Primetime then backed him into a corner, and leveled him with some forearms, before sending him to the turnbuckles on the opposite side. Chronos landed, and Primetime charged in shoulder first. Chronos moved however, and Primetime went flying into the ring post. Chronos then grabbed Primetime by the hair and pulled him out to the center of the ring. He then motioned that it was 'over', and he charged towards the corner. Chronosphere. No, Primetime countered and instead draped Chronos hard on the top rope. Primetime then shook off the shoulder first mishap, before hooking Chronos vertical suplex style. He held him in the air, before draping his legs across the ropes. Everyone knew what was coming up next. Snapshot. No, Chronos countered and fell behind Primetime, and wrapped him up with a waistlock. Primetime countered with a go-behind into a waistlock of his own. Primetime charged Chronos towards the ropes for a roll-up attempt, but Chronos held onto the ropes, leaving Primetime to roll backwards, somersault style. Primetime stood to his feet, and Chronos charged, taking him down with a simple dropkick. A cover gained only a two, but Chronos was on fire. Chronos brought PT up and sent him to the ropes. Primetime reversed again, and then ducked for a backdrop. Chronos however had other plans. Stopping behind Primetime, he hooked him for a reverse DDT. Lowfall. The crowd erupted in cheers, and Chronos quickly went for another cover. One. Two. No. Primetime kicked out. Undaunted, Chronos decided to go upstairs. Standing on the top rope, Chronos flew off with a frogsplash. He connected, and hooked the leg for another pinfall attempt. At two, Primetime again kicked out. Seeing as how he couldn't pin him, Chronos decided to try another way to defeat him. He brought Primetime up and sent him to the ropes with an Irish Whip. Primetime returned and Chronos wrapped him up with a sleeperhold. The Hourglass. The audience cheered, as it appeared Primetime would go 'nighty-night'. Instead, however, Primetime countered out of it by dropping down to the mat. Chronos went down with him, and his chin struck the top of the champion's head. This blow sent him backwards, and found himself in deep pain from the move. Primetime shook off the effects of the frogsplash, the Lowfall, and the Hourglass, before pulling Chronos towards a corner. Taking a page out of his mentor's book - the very man who had his career ended last night at the hands of Primetime - PT went upstairs. He leaped off. 450 Splash. Connected. The fans booed at Primetime's move, as it was not out of tribute - more out of contempt for a man the fans held with such high regard. Primetime hooked Chronos' leg, and the match appeared over. One. Two. No. Chronos got a foot on the nearby ropes, and Primetime screamed out an f-word or two in frustration. Frustrated, Primetime brought Chronos up and sent him flying through the ropes to the outside. He then joined him out there, grabbing the timekeeper and moving him off his chair. He then prepared to use that same steel chair. Chronos got to his feet, and Primetime swung at his back, but the referee prevented that at the last minute. As the two men engaged in a proverbial tug of war for the chair, Chronos turned around, saw what was going on, and broke it up with a dive. This brought Primetime and the referee towards each other; and resulted in a collision. Primetime went sprawling back onto the French announcers' table, while the referee crumpled to the floor in a heap. The challenger stood up and looked concerned for the referee, but he had a match to wrestle. An opponent to destroy. A championship... to win. Chronos slowly walked over to the French announcers' table, but not until after he picked up the same chair Primetime was going to use on him. Primetime saw Chronos coming towards him with the chair, and seeked to flee the area. He climbed up onto the table, and Chronos ran towards him after throwing the chair down, and joining Primetime up on the table. The two men fought back and forth, before Primetime got the upper hand with a low blow. Primetime then hooked Chronos for a Primetime Bomb. Instead of doing it to the table both men were on, he dropped Chronos through the one that was closest to him - the Spanish announcers' table. Chronos landed with a sickening crash, and the Spanish commentators moved out of the way as quickly as they could. Primetime stood with a pleased/evil smile on his face, but he too found himself on the floor, as the table he was standing on collapse. Primetime went through feet first, but fell at the end and landed on his chest. Both men lay in opposite positions. Primetime on his chest, and Chronos on his back. The cameras gave us a look down at Chronos, who had his eyes rolled back in his head. The scene was reminiscent of a fifty-two car pile-up on I-15. It wasn't over, however. Primetime got back to a vertical position, and looked down at the fallen Chronos. He decided he wasn't done. He grabbed the challenger and sent him up onto the American' announcers table, where only Jeremiah Frankton sat. Primetime hunched over his opponent, and assaulted him with punches to the face. Chronos seemed to have had enough. Primetime stood to his feet, and looked at the nearby crowd. He raised his arms in the air, and the fans gave him an ovation of boos not seen since a reaction like that was given to David Spencer... Austin Davis... or even Eron The Relentless. But while Primetime basked in the jeers from the Phillips Arena crowd, Chronos saw an opportunity. He returned the low-blow Primetime had given him earlier, and then spun him around, hooking him for a devastating move. Figure Four DDT. Right through the fucking table. Both men crashed, and the fans sat in awe over what they had just seen. Jeremiah Frankton tried to find some words to describe what he had just saw, but couldn't. Frankton sat speechless, and let the destruction speak for itself. A minute passed as both the reigning champion and challenger got back up. Chronos stood and walked around for a moment, seemingly in disbelief over what had just happened. He saw Primetime getting up, and rolled him into the ring. He then went for a cover, but the referee was still knocked silly from the collision earlier. Chronos stood to his feet, and looked down to the floor from inside the ring. The referee was just getting up. Chronos urged him to get in the ring, as in mind, the match was at his hand. The match was won. Until... a figure emerged from the crowd. The Black Panther. Panther spun Chronos around and kicked him in the midsection, before whipping him to the ropes. Chronos however reversed it, and the big man returned to spot Chronos ducking for a backdrop. Panther saw his opportunity. Pumphandle slam. Killer Instinct. The Panther laid out Chronos on the mat, before reviving Primetime, and draping his arm over Chronos. The fWo Internet Champion then exited the ring, and rolled the referee into the ring. Panther began to boast to the crowd that the match was over. The referee counted. One. Two. No! Chronos kicked out at two and a half, and the match was still on. Panther was flustered about it, and pulled the referee to the floor. The dazed referee stood and took in all the expletives and profane language Panther undoubtedly was giving the referee. Panther obviously felt it was a slow count. Meanwhile, back in the ring, the champion and challenger began to come to. About five feet away from each other in the ring, they saw each other getting up. Meanwhile, the referee had largely enough, and told the Panther to go backstage, or he would disqualify Primetime, and reward Chronos the World Title. With great reluctance, Panther found himself exiting to a chorus of boos. The referee turned back to the ring, and saw Chronos and Primetime up. They charged at each other, and both men went for the same move. A simultaneous double clothesline. Both men were now down, and the referee had no choice but to begin a ten count. One. Two. Three. Four. Primetime started showing signs of life. Five. Six. Seven. Eight. Nine. Primetime reached his feet, and slowly walked over to a corner, and he exited the ring. Climbing up to the top rope, he prepared for his next move - but he took too long, and a groggy Chronos fell into the ropes, which in turn crotched Primetime. Chronos slowly pulled himself up, and joined Primetime in the corner. He climbed to the second rope, and hooked him for the same devastating move that he won the 2000 Wrestler of the Year tournament with against Crusader. The Figure Four DDT - from the top rope. But Primetime knew what was coming, and seeked to escape it. He did, and then hooked Chronos for the Primetime Bomb off the top rope - but Chronos backdropped out of it. Primetime held on for the sunset flip, and the referee went to the mat to count. One. Two. No! Chronos broke up the pin, and both men were now running on adrenaline. They both came to, and Chronos charged towards Primetime, kicking him in the stomach. He then grabbed him with a headlock, and hooked him for his other finisher, the same move that Primetime countered earlier in the night. Chronosphere. No, Primetime countered, and again crotched him in the middle part of the top rope. Chronos writhed in pain, as his nether regions were certainly now tender. Primetime sought to end the match the same way he did their last singles encounter, although it didn't work earlier tonight. He grabbed him suplex style, draped his legs on the ropes, and brought him back to complete the move. Slingshot Michinoku Driver. The Snapshot. It was over. Primetime crawled over about twenty seconds later to make the cover. The referee positioned himself. One. Two. Oh hell no. Chronos got his shoulder up and Primetime was infuriated. This was the same move that defeated Chronos on November 19th - not even a month ago. Why not now? Primetime figured it was due to a slow three count, and told the referee that much. He backed him in the corner and berated the referee, who wasn't taking shit from anyone, the least of which Primetime. Primetime shoved the referee, who responded with a shove his own, much to the delight of the crowd. Primetime shoved the referee again, who went flying back into the corner. Happy at what he had done, Primetime went to turn back to face the challenger. Who was not only back on his feet, he was charging at Primetime with full speed. Primetime moved out of the way and Chronos charged right into the referee instead. He squashed the referee accidentally, and moved out of his way. The referee stumbled out of the corner, and flopped much like Ric Flair would on the mat. Chronos turn and saw Primetime, and moved towards him. Primetime was ready. Kick to the stomach. Primetime Bomb, only this time in the center of the ring. Primetime covered him, but the referee was out of it. No matter. "Dragula" by Rob Zombie hit. Primetime turned his attention to the stage as the fans ovated, cheering wildly, as they knew who was coming out. Primetime knew too... and just in case you didn't, it was the man who was supposed to be wrestling Primetime tonight. Mike Bear. He finally had arrived... albeit too late. With a look of despair on his eyes, Primetime practically frothed at mouth, looking to the stage anxiously. He waited for Bear to come out onto the stage, but Bear never appeared. Well, in Primetime's view anyway. Bear was now in the ring, and he spun Primetime around, quickly placing him on his back, and taking him out with the Whiplash. Surely, this would keep Primetime down. Bear grabbed Chronos and placed him atop the champion, before trying to revive the referee. The referee began to show signs of life, so Bear retreated backstage. Panther had come out onto the stage. Without hesitation or reluctance, the two began brawling, but their brawl took it's way backstage. Forty seconds passed before the referee finally recovered enough to make the count. One. Two. At two and 99 ?/100ths, Primetime seemingly kicked out. The fans gasped. Chronos began to get up, and the fans clapped in support of the challenger - seemingly pushing him to beat the man they so despised. Primetime too was getting up... and so was the referee, albeit more slowly than the athletes themselves. Primetime turned, and quickly was taking into the corner for an inverted Acid Drop. Chronosphere. This time, he hit it. The third time was indeed the charm. But could Chronos make the cover? Chronos covered the champion, and hooked a leg for good measure. The referee counted. One. Two. Three. New fWo World Champion. The Phillips Arena' foundation was rocked with cheers from the crowd, as someone had finally put an end to the near two month title reign of Primetime. Primetime remained on the mat, staring up at the lights in the rafters of the Ted Turner funded establishment. The referee handed the World Title to Ric Chronos who took it to a corner, and held it in the air for everyone to see. The fans continued to cheer, before Mike Bear reappeared, apparently having outlasted The Black Panther. Chronos turned back and saw Bear, and the two acknowledged each other with a stare. It was a given that in twelve days at Countdown To Oblivion 3, these two men would meet for the fWo World Title. As Chronos & Bear stood in a standoff, time was already ticking. Mike Bear was more than ready for that night, but would Ric Chronos be? Chronos had just come off two grueling matches within the span of twenty four hours, and would have less than two weeks to prepare for his first title defense. But for a man who recovered from a career threatening injury in less than two months, two weeks was more than enough time for the 'Man Of The Hour'. The final live Slaughter of 2000 came to a close, with Frankton reminding everyone to tune in next Sunday for the fWo's Best Of 2000 show. The End Of 2000 Forthcoming - Countdown To Oblivion Looms
